Hi, doctor. Thank you for seeing me. Um... well, I guess I came in because... I don't know, it's probably not a huge deal, but I just keep beating myself up a lot lately.

Um, I don't know... I really hope so. I mean, I want to feel like I belong there. But right now... 

I know I'm probably frustrating you by saying this again, and I'm really sorry, but... it's just really hard to imagine feeling any different. I just constantly feel like I've fooled everyone into thinking I can handle all this, and eventually, they're going to figure it out.

I mean, from what I've been telling you, do you think it's possible for someone like me to actually get past this? I just want to make sure I'm not a hopeless case or anything...

I just don't want to make it seem like my whole life is falling apart, because it's not. It's just... I feel really guilty about letting them down at the office. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things outside of your work performance?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try to be a good husband, a good friend... I don't really walk around feeling guilty about my personal life. I hope that's okay to say? I don't want to sound like I think I'm perfect or anything, because I definitely make mistakes! \n\nBut... yeah, it's really just the work thing. Like I mentioned before, that's the whole reason I'm here. I just... I keep beating myself up over it. I feel so guilty, like I'm not actually senior material and I somehow tricked everyone at the office into thinking I could handle all these responsibilities. I just feel bad that I'm letting them down. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about things that might happen in the future when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I like to think I'm pretty level-headed about the future, you know? \n\nIt really does just come back to the job.
